Company Name: Con Edison 
Case Description:  2019 Con Ed Electric & Gas Rate Filings 
Case: 19-E-0065, 19-G-0066 
 
 
Response to EDF Interrogatories – Set  EDF-6 
Date of Response: 8/2/2019 
Responding Witness: Gas Infrastructure Operations & Supply Panel 
 
 
Question No. : 48 
 
  
Please refer to the Queens Transmission Upgrade White Paper (NESE Denied), which asserts 
that, if the Williams Northeast Supply Enhancement (“NESE”)  project is not built, “with the 
forecasted increase in customer demand, there would not be enough pipeline supplies to meet the 
combined New York Facilities system demand except from the existing supply-line in lower 
Manhattan by the winter of 2020/21….” 
a)      When did Con Edison first identify that the ability to serve load growth in New 
York City depended on approval of the NESE project? 
b)      When did Con Edson first notify the Commission and/or Staff that the ability to 
serve load growth in New York City depended on approval of the NESE project? 
c)      Has Con Edison conducted an analysis of the connection between the Company’s 
ability to serve load growth in New York City and the NESE project? If yes, please 
provide that analysis.   
d)      Please provide all documents and/or analyses addressing the connection between 
the Company’s ability to serve load growth in New York City and the NESE project.  
e)      Has Con Edison conducted an analysis to assess whether contracting for NESE 
capacity would or would not be lower cost than the Queens Transmission Upgrade 
project? If yes, please provide that analysis. 
 
 
Response 
 
a) First, the Company notes that its decision to propose additional projects (i.e., both the 
Queens and Manhattan projects) did not solely depend upon whether Williams Northeast 
Supply Enhancement (“NESE”) project was approved.  The Company has also made 
clear that the timing and nature of these projects would change depending upon whether 
NESE was approved or denied.   
 
Each year, the Company and NGRID (together “Companies”) prepare joint hydraulic models of 
the NY Facilities System.  Starting with the models prepared in the Spring/Summer of 2018, the 
Companies prepared models for the forward five winter periods, with and without the NESE 
project entering service for the winter 2020/2021, in accordance with Williams’ schedule. The 
Exhibit__(GL-4)
Page 1 of 3


--- Page 218 ---
Page 2 of 3 
Spring/Summer of 2018 modelling exercise showed that without NESE it would be difficult to 
meet firm peak gas demand in NYC, but the planning assumption was that NESE would be in 
service in accordance with Williams’ schedule.  
 
The assumption was based on the expectation that NESE would receive the required permits on a 
timely basis.  Con Edison is not a shipper on this project, and based its expectations on its 
discussions with NGRID and Williams.   
 
In early 2019, however, this outlook began to change. NGRID communicated to the Company 
that it had begun taking steps to limit firm customer demand growth should the NESE project not 
be approved, because of growing uncertainty around the approval of state water permits.  
 
In addition, when FERC issued its final Environmental Impact Statement for NESE at the end of 
January, parties opposed to NESE increased their focus on the pending state approvals in both NJ 
and NY.  At the same time, New York State in particular announced its intention to pursue more 
aggressive policies geared toward reducing greenhouse gas emissions. 
 
As a result, the Company began an intensive engineering review of how it could postpone the 
need for a moratorium in the NYC area under a scenario that assumed NESE did not enter 
service but NGRID’s future peak demand growth ceased.  The projects mentioned by the 
Company in the April Preliminary Update were the result of that review. 
 
The ability of these projects to enable the Company to meet the most recent forecast for firm 
peak demand by procuring more delivered services on existing pipelines until the announced 
Iroquois compressor project enters service for the winter 2023-2024 was confirmed by the 
preliminary joint hydraulic models of the NY Facilities system prepared by CECONY and 
NGRID in the past month.  
 
b) The Company has continually indicated in publicly filed documents since September 
2017 that moratoriums may be necessary to continue meeting firm peak demand if a 
particular region was projected to exceed available supply.  The Company has been 
keeping DPS Staff informed with respect to the potential for moratoriums 
contemporaneous with its public filings.

--- Page 219 ---
Page 3 of 3 
c) Please see response to part a). 
 
d) The joint hydraulic models referenced in the response to subpart b) are confidential 
critical infrastructure information.  Please see the Company’s rebuttal testimony and 
associated white papers discussing the Queens and Manhattan Transmission Projects. 
 
e) Given the Company and NGRID jointly own the NY Facilities system, the impact of 
NESE on the downstate Gas Supply versus firm peak demand imbalance is unchanged by 
which Company contracts for the capacity.  Accordingly, even if the project volume had 
been split between Con Edison and NGRID, the Queens project would still be required.

Question No. : 50 
 
  
 
Please refer to the Gas Infrastructure, Operations and Supply Panel Update/Rebuttal Testimony 
at page 136, which states that each year “Con Edison and the two downstate National Grid 
companies create a joint hydraulic flow model to determine the citygate supply deliveries 
required to meet peak firm demand under design conditions.”   
a)      Does the Company submit the results of this modeling exercise to the Commission 
and/or Staff for review and/or approval?  
b)      Has this joint modeling exercise of the New York Facilities identified that there may 
not be enough pipeline supplies to meet the combined New York Facilities system 
demand?  If yes, please provide such analysis and the date of such analysis.

Edison’s White Paper on the Queens Transmission Upgrade (NESE denied) White Paper 
at page 1, which notes that the modeling and forecasting of the combined New York 
Facilities demand will be completed this summer? If no, please explain the differences 
between these two modeling processes. 
 
 
Response

and NGRID (“Companies”), the Companies will file an updated appendix annually with 
the PSC.  In addition, the Company reviews the results of hydraulic modeling of the NY 
Facilities system as well as its high, medium and low pressure systems with DPS Staff 
from time to time at their request. 
b) Please see responses to EDF-6-48(a) & (d).   
c) Yes.
